(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{P})
satisfies
Property
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{prod}})
if it verifies the following assertions.

(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{prod}})_{1}

There exist measured subgroupoids
\mathcal{A},\mathcal{N}\subseteq\mathcal{P}
such that

(a)

\mathcal{A}
is amenable and of infinite type, and is stably normalized by
\mathcal{N}
;

(b)

\mathcal{N}
is everywhere non-amenable, and is stably normalized by
\mathcal{P}
;

(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{prod}})_{2}

Whenever
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}
is a measured subgroupoid of
\mathcal{G}
that satisfies Property
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{prod}})_{1}
, and such that
\mathcal{P}
is stably contained in
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}
, then
\mathcal{P}
and
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}
are stably equal.

Remark 7.3
.

We mention the following crucial stabilities for Property
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{prod}})
.

1.

If
(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{P})
satisfies Property
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{prod}})
, then for every positive measure Borel subset
U\subseteq X
, the pair
(\mathcal{G}_{|U},\mathcal{P}_{|U})
also satisfies Property
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{prod}})
.

2.

If there exists a Borel partition
X^{*}=\sqcup_{i\in I}X_{i}
of a conull Borel subset
X^{*}\subseteq X
into at most countably many Borel subsets, such that for every
i\in I
, the pair
(\mathcal{G}_{|X_{i}},\mathcal{P}_{|X_{i}})
satisfies Property
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{prod}})
, then
(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{P})
satisfies Property
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{prod}})
.

These stabilities will allow us to take restrictions and work up to a countable Borel partition in the proofs below. All other properties that will appear later in this section (Properties
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{spec}}),(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{cofact}}),(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{fact}}),(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{vert}})
) and the next two also satisfy the same stabilities.

Property
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{prod}})
is the groupoid-theoretic translation of the following idea: if
P=F_{1}\times\dots\times F_{k}
is a maximal direct product in
G
, then (unless possibly if this product comes from a clique and no factor contains an infinite amenable subgroup) there exists an infinite amenable subgroup
A
in some
F_{i}
, normalized by
N=F_{1}\times\dots\times\hat{F}_{j}\times\dots\times F_{k}
, which is itself normalized by
P
. It turns out that conversely, maximal subgroups of
G
with this property are either product parabolic subgroups, or else they could be contained in a parabolic subgroup whose type is an isolated clique. The following lemma, which is inspired from
[
HHI23
, Lemma 5.4]
, establishes this fact at the groupoid-theoretic level.

Lemma 7.4
.

Let
G
be a graph product of countably infinite groups over a finite simple graph
\Gamma
.
Let
\mathcal{G}
be a measured groupoid over a standard probability space
X
, equipped with an action-like cocycle
\rho:\mathcal{G}\to G
. Let
\mathcal{P}\subseteq\mathcal{G}
be a measured subgroupoid. Then

1.

If
(\mathcal{P},\rho)
is of maximal product type and nowhere of isolated clique type, then
(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{P})
satisfies Property
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{prod}})
.

2.

If
(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{P})
satisfies Property
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{prod}})
, then there exists a Borel partition
X=X^{1}\sqcup X^{2}
such that
(\mathcal{P}_{|X^{1}},\rho)
is of maximal product type, and
\mathcal{P}_{|X^{2}}
is stably contained in a subgroupoid
\mathcal{Q}
of
\mathcal{G}_{|X^{2}}
such that
(\mathcal{Q},\rho)
is of isolated clique type.

Remark 7.5
.

When
\Gamma
does not contain any isolated clique, then (as in
[
HHI23
]
) the above lemma gives a characterization of pairs
(\mathcal{P},\rho)
of maximal product type that is independent of the choice of an action-like cocycle
\rho:\mathcal{G}\to G
. But when
\Gamma
has isolated cliques, whether or not subgroupoids of isolated clique type satisfy
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{prod}})
depends on the vertex groups. This is why there is an asymmetry in the statement of the lemma.

In particular, we mention that with our definitions, it is unclear to us whether or not a subgroupoid of the form
\rho^{-1}(C)
, where
C
is a parabolic subgroup associated to an isolated clique on several vertices, with nonamenable vertex groups, always satisfies Property
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{prod}})
. This is the case as soon as one of the vertex groups contains an infinite amenable subgroup
A
, by taking
\mathcal{A}=\rho^{-1}(A)
and
\mathcal{N}=\rho^{-1}(N)
, where
N
is the direct product of the other vertex groups of the clique. The difficulty comes from the case where the vertex groups are torsion groups, e.g. free Burnside groups. This uncertainty required us to make a few adjustments in the statements and in the arguments.

Proof.

Step 1
Let us first prove that if
(\mathcal{P},\rho)
is of maximal product type and nowhere of isolated clique type, then
(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{P})
satisfies Property
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{prod}})_{1}
.

Up to passing to a conull Borel subset of
X
and performing a countable Borel partition of
X
, we can assume that
\mathcal{P}=\rho^{-1}(P)
for some maximal product parabolic subgroup
P
not of isolated clique type. Write
P=P_{1}\times P_{2}
as a product of two infinite parabolic subgroups. Up to changing the roles of
P_{1}
and
P_{2}
, Lemma
6.5
,
6.5
lets us assume that
P_{2}
contains a non-abelian free subgroup. If
P_{1}
is amenable, then the conclusion follows by letting
\mathcal{A}=\rho^{-1}(P_{1})
and
\mathcal{N}=\rho^{-1}(P_{2})
: indeed

—

\mathcal{A}
is amenable because
A
is and
\rho
has trivial kernel (Lemma
4.7
,
4.7
), of infinite type because
A
is infinite and
\rho
is action-like, and (stably) normalized by
\mathcal{N}
because
A
is normalized by
P_{2}
(Example
4.6
,
4.6
).

—

\mathcal{N}
is everywhere non-amenable because
P_{2}
is non-amenable and
\rho
is action-like, and
\mathcal{N}
is (stably) normalized by
\mathcal{P}
because
N
is normalized by
P
(Example
4.6
).

Otherwise, if
P_{1}
is non-amenable, let
A\subseteq P_{2}
be an infinite cyclic subgroup; then the conclusion follows by letting
\mathcal{A}=\rho^{-1}(A)
and
\mathcal{N}=\rho^{-1}(P_{1})
.

Step 2
Let us prove that if
\mathcal{P}
satisfies
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{prod}})_{1}
, then there exist a countable partition
X^{*}=\sqcup_{i\in I}X_{i}
of a conull Borel subset
X^{*}\subseteq X
into at most countably many Borel subsets
X_{i}
, and for every
i\in I
, a parabolic subgroup
Q_{i}
which is either a product or of isolated clique type, such that
\mathcal{P}_{|X_{i}}\subseteq\rho^{-1}(Q_{i})_{|X_{i}}
.

Let
\mathcal{A},\mathcal{N}\subseteq\mathcal{P}
be measured subgroupoids given by
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{prod}})_{1}
. Up to replacing
X
by a conull Borel subset and taking a countable Borel partition, we will assume that

—

\mathcal{A}
is normalized by
\mathcal{N}
, and
\mathcal{N}
is normalized by
\mathcal{P}
;

—

(\mathcal{A},\rho)
is tightly
A
-supported for some parabolic subgroup
A\subseteq G
(Lemma
4.16
,
4.16
) – notice that
A\neq\{1\}
because
\mathcal{A}
is of infinite type and
\rho
has trivial kernel;

—

(\mathcal{N},\rho)
is tightly
N
-supported for some parabolic subgroup
N\subseteq G
(Lemma
4.16
) – notice that
N
is non-amenable because
\mathcal{N}
is everywhere non-amenable and
\rho
has trivial kernel, see Lemma
4.7
,
4.7
;

—

\mathcal{N}\subseteq\rho^{-1}(A\times A^{\perp})
and
\mathcal{P}\subseteq\rho^{-1}(N\times N^{\perp})
using the invariance of the support by the normalizer (Lemma
4.17
).

Notice in particular that
N\subseteq A\times A^{\perp}
, by definition of the support. Now there are two cases.

—

Case 1: Either
N
splits as a direct product of two infinite parabolic subgroups, or
N^{\perp}\neq\{1\}
. In this case
Q:=N\times N^{\perp}
is a product parabolic subgroup, we are done since
\mathcal{P}\subseteq\rho^{-1}(Q)
.

—

Case 2:
N
does not split as a direct product of two infinite parabolic subgroups, and
N^{\perp}=\{1\}
. Then
\mathcal{P}\subseteq\rho^{-1}(N)
, and we will now show that in this case
N
is of isolated clique type, which will conclude. As
N\subseteq A\times A^{\perp}
, we deduce using Lemma
2.7
,
2.7
that
N=(N\cap A)\times(N\cap A^{\perp})
. But we assumed that
N
does not split as a direct product, so either
N\subseteq A
or
N\subseteq A^{\perp}
. If the latter holds we have
\{1\}\subsetneq A\subseteq N^{\perp}
which contradicts our assumption that
N^{\perp}
is trivial. Hence
N\subseteq A
, and
A^{\perp}=\{1\}
because
N^{\perp}=\{1\}
. Lemma
6.15
,
6.15
(applied to the amenable groupoid
\mathcal{A}
, which is normalized by the non-amenable groupoid
\mathcal{N}
) thus ensures that the clique factor
A_{0}
of
A
is non-amenable, and
N\cap A_{0}\neq\{1\}
. As
N\subseteq A
, as
N\cap A_{0}\neq\{1\}
, as above using again Lemma
2.7
,
2.7
, and that
N
does not split as a direct product we obtain that

N
is equal to a conjugate of a vertex group. Since
N^{\perp}=\{1\}
, this vertex group is isolated. This completes the proof in Case 2.

Step 3
We now prove that if
(\mathcal{P},\rho)
is of maximal product type, then
(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{P})
satisfies Property
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{prod}})_{2}
: this will complete the proof of the first part of the lemma.

Let
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}
be a measured subgroupoid of
\mathcal{G}
that satisfies Property
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{prod}})_{1}
, such that
\mathcal{P}
is stably contained in
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}
. By Step 2, we can find a countable partition
X^{*}=\sqcup_{i\in I}X_{i}
of a conull Borel subset
X^{*}\subseteq X
into at most countably many Borel subsets
X_{i}
, and for every
i\in I
, a parabolic subgroup
Q_{i}
which is either a product or of isolated clique type, such that
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{|X_{i}}\subseteq\rho^{-1}(Q_{i})_{|X_{i}}
. Without loss of generality, we will assume that all subsets
X_{i}
have positive measure. Since
\mathcal{P}
is stably contained in
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}
, up to passing again to a conull Borel subset and performing a countable partition, we will assume that
\mathcal{P}_{|X_{i}}\subseteq\rho^{-1}(Q_{i})_{|X_{i}}
.
Since
(\mathcal{P},\rho)
is of maximal product type, up to a further partition again, we have
\mathcal{P}_{|X_{i}}=\rho^{-1}(P_{i})_{|X_{i}}
for some maximal product parabolic subgroup
P_{i}
. It thus follows from Lemma
4.14
,
4.14
that
P_{i}\subseteq Q_{i}
. We now observe that in fact
P_{i}=Q_{i}
. Indeed, either
Q_{i}
is a product and then this follows from the maximality of
P_{i}
. Or
Q_{i}
is of isolated clique type and contains the product parabolic subgroup
P_{i}
, then the corresponding clique is not reduced to a point; so
Q_{i}
is a product and as above
P_{i}=Q_{i}
.
It follows from the above that
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}
is stably contained in
\mathcal{P}
, showing that
\mathcal{P}
satisfies Property
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{prod}})_{2}
.

Step 4
We finally prove the second assertion of the lemma.

So assume that
(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{P})
satisfies Property
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{prod}})
. By Step 2, there exist a countable partition
X^{*}=\sqcup_{i\in I}X_{i}
of a conull Borel subset
X^{*}\subseteq X
into at most countably many Borel subsets
X_{i}
, and for every
i\in I
, a parabolic subgroup
Q_{i}
which is either of product type or of isolated clique type, such that
\mathcal{P}_{|X_{i}}\subseteq\rho^{-1}(Q_{i})_{|X_{i}}
. Up to increasing
Q_{i}
, we will assume that
Q_{i}
is either a maximal product or of isolated clique type (in particular
Q_{i}
is not a proper subgroup of a parabolic subgroup of isolated clique type). Let
I_{2}\subseteq I
be the subset consisting of all
i
such that
Q_{i}
is of isolated clique type, and let
I_{1}=I\setminus I_{2}
. Let
X^{1}
(resp.
X^{2}
) be the union of all
X_{i}
with
i\in I_{1}
(resp. with
i\in I_{2}
). Let us now prove that
\mathcal{P}_{|X_{i}}
is actually (stably) equal to
\rho^{-1}(Q_{i})_{|X_{i}}
for all
i\in I_{1}
. So let
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}
be a subgroupoid of
\mathcal{G}
such that for every
i\in I_{1}
, one has
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{|X_{i}}=\rho^{-1}(Q_{i})_{|X_{i}}
, with
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{|X^{2}}=\mathcal{P}_{|X^{2}}
. Then
\mathcal{P}
is stably contained in
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}
. In addition
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}
satisfies Property
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{prod}})_{1}
: this uses Step 1 of this proof for
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{|X^{1}}
, and the fact that
\mathcal{P}
satisfies Property
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{prod}})
for
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{|X^{2}}
. So
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{prod}})_{2}
for
(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{P})
implies that
\mathcal{P}
and
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}
are stably equal. In particular the second assertion of the lemma follows with
\mathcal{Q}
a groupoid verifying
\mathcal{Q}_{|X_{i}}:=\rho^{-1}(Q_{i})_{|X_{i}}
for every
i\in I_{2}
.
∎

7.3
Recognizing factors in products

Special subproducts.

Let
P=\mathsf{F}_{1}\times\dots\times\mathsf{F}_{n}
be a product parabolic subgroup, where the subgroups
\mathsf{F}_{j}
with
j\geq 1
are parabolic subgroups that do not further split as direct products of parabolic subgroups (in particular the clique factor of
P
is fully decomposed). A
subproduct
is a subgroup of
P
equal to the product of finitely many (possibly only one) factors
\mathsf{F}_{j}
. A subproduct
S
of
P
is
special within
G
if
N_{G}(S)\supsetneq N_{G}(P)
,
that is to say
S\times S^{\perp}\supsetneq P\times P^{\perp}
.

Let now
\mathcal{G}
be a measured groupoid over a standard probability space
X
, and let
\rho:\mathcal{G}\to G
be an action-like cocycle. Let
\mathcal{P}
be a measured subgroupoid such that
(\mathcal{P},\rho)
is of product type. Let
X^{*}=\sqcup_{i\in I}X_{i}
be a partition of a conull Borel subset
X^{*}\subseteq X
, and for every
i\in I
,
P_{i}
be a parabolic subgroup of
G
of product type, such that
\mathcal{P}_{|X_{i}}=\rho^{-1}(P_{i})_{|X_{i}}
. Let
\mathcal{S}
be a measured subgroupoid of
\mathcal{P}
. We say that
(\mathcal{S},\rho)
is
of special subproduct type within
(\mathcal{P},\rho)
if, up to passing to a further conull Borel subset and refining the above partition, for every
i\in I
, there exists a special subproduct
S_{i}
of
P_{i}
such that
\mathcal{S}_{|X_{i}}=\rho^{-1}(S_{i})_{|X_{i}}
. We observe that this definition does not depend on the decomposition of
X
we chose.

We highlight the following property, which translates the notion of a special subproduct at the groupoid-theoretic level.

Definition 7.6

(Property
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{spec}})
)
.

Let
\mathcal{G}
be a measured groupoid over a standard probability space
X
, and let
\mathcal{S}\subseteq\mathcal{P}
be two measured subgroupoids of
\mathcal{G}
. We say that the triple
(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{P},\mathcal{S})
satisfies
Property
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{spec}})
if the following two properties hold.

(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{spec}})_{1}

The subgroupoid
\mathcal{S}
is stably normal in
\mathcal{P}
.

(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{spec}})_{2}

There exists a measured subgroupoid
\mathcal{N}\subseteq\mathcal{G}
that stably normalizes
\mathcal{S}
, such that for every measured subgroupoid
\mathcal{N}^{\prime}
of
\mathcal{G}
that stably normalizes
\mathcal{P}
, and every Borel subset
U\subseteq X
of positive measure, one has
\mathcal{N}_{|U}\nsubseteq\mathcal{N}^{\prime}_{|U}
.

Lemma 7.7
.

Let
G
be a graph product of countably infinite groups over a finite simple graph
\Gamma
. Let
\mathcal{G}
be a measured groupoid over a standard probability space
X
, equipped with an action-like cocycle
\rho:\mathcal{G}\to G
. Let
\mathcal{S}\subseteq\mathcal{P}\subseteq\mathcal{G}
be measured subgroupoids, with
(\mathcal{P},\rho)
of product type.

1.

If
(\mathcal{S},\rho)
is of special subproduct type within
(\mathcal{P},\rho)
, then
(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{P},\mathcal{S})
satisfies Property
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{spec}})
.

2.

If
(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{P},\mathcal{S})
satisfies Property
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{spec}})
, then there exist a countable Borel partition
X^{*}=\sqcup_{i\in I}X_{i}
of a conull Borel subset
X^{*}\subseteq X
, and for every
i\in I
, a product parabolic subgroup
P_{i}
, and a special subproduct
S_{i}\subseteq P_{i}
, such that
\mathcal{P}_{|X_{i}}=\rho^{-1}(P_{i})_{|X_{i}}
and
(\mathcal{S}_{|X_{i}},\rho)
is tightly
S_{i}
-supported.

Remark that in our definition of
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{spec}})
– in contrast with
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{prod}})
– we do not have any maximality condition. This explains the asymmetry in the above lemma.

Proof.

Without loss of generality, we can assume that
\mathcal{P}=\rho^{-1}(P)
for some product parabolic subgroup
P\subseteq G
.

We first prove Assertion 1, so assume that
(\mathcal{S},\rho)
is of special subproduct type within
(\mathcal{P},\rho)
. That
(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{P},\mathcal{S})
satisfies
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{spec}})_{1}
follows from the fact that subproducts are normal in the ambient product, and Example
4.6
,
4.6
. We now prove that it satisfies
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{spec}})_{2}
. Up to passing to a conull Borel subset and taking a countable partition of
X
, we will assume that
\mathcal{S}=\rho^{-1}(S)
for some special subproduct
S\subseteq P
. Let
\mathcal{N}=\rho^{-1}(S\times S^{\perp})
, which normalizes
\mathcal{S}
. Let
\mathcal{N}^{\prime}
be a measured subgroupoid of
\mathcal{G}
that stably normalizes
\mathcal{P}
. Let
U\subseteq X
be a positive measure Borel subset. Since
(\mathcal{P},\rho)
is tightly
P
-supported (Lemma
4.15
,
4.15
) and stably normalized by
\mathcal{N}^{\prime}
, Lemma
4.17
,
4.17
ensures that there exists a positive measure Borel subset
V\subseteq U
such that
\mathcal{N}^{\prime}_{|V}\subseteq\rho^{-1}(P\times P^{\perp})_{|V}
. Since
S
is a special subproduct, we have
S\times S^{\perp}\nsubseteq P\times P^{\perp}
. It thus follows from Lemma
4.14
,
4.14
that
\mathcal{N}_{|V}\nsubseteq\mathcal{N}^{\prime}_{|V}
; in particular
\mathcal{N}_{|U}\nsubseteq\mathcal{N}^{\prime}_{|U}
, so
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{spec}})_{2}
holds.

We now prove the second assertion, so assume that
(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{P},\mathcal{S})
satisfies Property
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{spec}})
. Without loss of generality, we can assume that
(\mathcal{S},\rho)
is tightly
S
-supported for some parabolic subgroup
S\subseteq P
. Since
\mathcal{S}
is stably normal in
\mathcal{P}
(by
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{spec}})_{1}
),
4.17
,
4.17
, ensures that
\mathcal{P}=\rho^{-1}(P)
is stably contained in
\rho^{-1}(S\times S^{\perp})
, and
4.14
,
4.14
ensures that
P\subseteq S\times S^{\perp}
. Therefore
S
is a subproduct of
P
(see
2.7
).
Let
\mathcal{N}
be a measured groupoid as in
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{spec}})_{2}
. Let
\mathcal{N}^{\prime}=\rho^{-1}(P\times P^{\perp})
, which normalizes
\mathcal{P}
. Since
\mathcal{N}
stably normalizes
\mathcal{S}
, by Lemma
4.17
, there exists a positive measure Borel subset
U\subseteq X
such that
\mathcal{N}_{|U}\subseteq\rho^{-1}(S\times S^{\perp})_{|U}
. It follows from
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{spec}})_{2}
that
S\times S^{\perp}
is not contained in
P\times P^{\perp}
. So
S
is a special subproduct, which completes the proof.
∎

Clique-inclusive co-factors.

Let
P
be a parabolic subgroup of product type. Write
P=C_{0}\times{F}_{1}\times\dots\times{F}_{k}
as a direct product of parabolic subgroups, where the
F_{j}
do not further split as a direct product of proper parabolic subgroups and
C_{0}
denotes the clique factor of
P
. A
clique-inclusive co-factor
of
P
is a subgroup of the form
C_{0}\times F_{1}\times\dots\times\hat{{F}}_{j}\times\dots\times F_{k}
for some
j\in\{1,\dots,k\}
. By convention, if
P=C_{0}
, there is no clique-inclusive co-factor.

Let now
\mathcal{G}
be a measured groupoid over a standard probability space
X
, and let
\rho:\mathcal{G}\to G
be an action-like cocycle. Let
\mathcal{P}
be a measured subgroupoid such that
(\mathcal{P},\rho)
is of product type. Let
X^{*}=\sqcup_{i\in I}X_{i}
be a partition of a conull Borel subset
X^{*}\subseteq X
, and for every
i\in I
,
P_{i}
be a product parabolic subgroup of
G
, such that
\mathcal{P}_{|X_{i}}=\rho^{-1}(P_{i})_{|X_{i}}
. Let
\mathcal{Q}
be a measured subgroupoid of
\mathcal{P}
. We say that
(\mathcal{Q},\rho)
is
of clique-inclusive co-factor type within
(\mathcal{P},\rho)
if, up to passing to a further conull Borel subset and refining the above partition, for every
i\in I
, there exists a clique-inclusive co-factor
Q_{i}
of
P_{i}
such that
\mathcal{Q}_{|X_{i}}=\rho^{-1}(Q_{i})_{|X_{i}}
.

We now aim to give a characterization of subgroupoids of clique-inclusive co-factor type. For this, we highlight the following property, an informal explanation is given below it.

Definition 7.8

(Property
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{cofact}})
)
.

Let
\mathcal{G}
be a measured groupoid over a standard probability space
X
. Let
\mathcal{Q}\subseteq\mathcal{P}
be measured subgroupoids of
\mathcal{G}
. We say that the triple
(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q})
satisfies
Property
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{cofact}})
if the following three properties hold.

(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{cofact}})_{1}

The groupoid
\mathcal{Q}
is stably normal in
\mathcal{P}
.

(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{cofact}})_{2}

There exists a measured subgroupoid
\mathcal{B}\subseteq\mathcal{P}
of infinite type such that

(a)

the groupoid
\mathcal{B}
is stably normalized by a measured subgroupoid
\mathcal{N}
of
\mathcal{G}
such that

i.

whenever
\mathcal{N}^{\prime}
is a measured subgroupoid of
\mathcal{G}
that stably normalizes
\mathcal{P}
, for every positive measure Borel subset
U\subseteq X
, one has
\mathcal{N}_{|U}\nsubseteq\mathcal{N}^{\prime}_{|U}
;

ii.

for every positive measure Borel subset
U\subseteq X
, if
\mathcal{B}_{|U}
is contained in a measured subgroupoid
\mathcal{S}
of
\mathcal{P}_{|U}
such that
(\mathcal{G}_{|U},\mathcal{P}_{|U},\mathcal{S})
satisfies Property
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{spec}})
, and if
\mathcal{N}^{\prime}
is a measured subgroupoid of
\mathcal{G}_{|U}
that stably normalizes
\mathcal{S}
, then for every positive measure Borel subset
V\subseteq U
, one has
\mathcal{N}_{|V}\nsubseteq\mathcal{N}^{\prime}_{|V}
;

(b)

the groupoid
\mathcal{Q}
stably normalizes
\mathcal{B}
.

(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{cofact}})_{3}

Whenever
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}
is a measured subgroupoid of
\mathcal{P}
such that
(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q}^{\prime})
satisfies Properties
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{cofact}})_{1}
and
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{cofact}})_{2}
, and such that
\mathcal{Q}
is stably contained in
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}
, then
\mathcal{Q}
and
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}
are stably equal.

The idea behind Property
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{cofact}})
is the following. Let
P=F_{1}\times\dots\times F_{n}
be a product parabolic subgroup, and for simplicity let us assume that its clique factor is trivial. Let
j\in\{1,\dots,n\}
. The group
Q=F_{1}\times\dots\times\hat{F}_{j}\times\dots\times F_{n}
is normal in
P
, as in
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{cofact}})_{1}
. Under our assumption that the defining graph
\Gamma
is strongly reduced, the subgraph
\Lambda_{j}
that gives the type of
F_{j}
is not collapsible. We can therefore find
B\subseteq F_{j}
, conjugate to a vertex group, whose normalizer is not contained in the normalizer of
F_{j}
. In particular
N_{G}(B)
is not contained in
N_{G}(P)
(as expressed in its groupoid-theoretic form by
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{cofact}})_{2}(a.i)
). And if
F_{j}
is contained in a special subproduct
S
, then
N_{G}(B)
is also not contained in
N_{G}(S)
(as expressed in its groupoid-theoretic form by
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{cofact}})_{2}(a.ii)
). Finally notice that
B
is normalized by
Q
, as in
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{cofact}})_{2}(b)
. Conversely, we will prove (in a groupoid-theoretic context) that these properties are enough to ensure that a subgroup(oid) is contained in one of co-factor type. And the maximality property
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{cofact}})_{3}
is here to ensure that we get the full co-factor, not just a proper subgroup(oid). (In the case where the clique factor of
P
is non-trivial, the above remains true, except that it now only provides a characterization of the co-factors which are clique-inclusive.) This is the contents of our next lemma, which as explained above crucially uses the assumption that the underlying graph
\Gamma
is strongly reduced.

Lemma 7.9
.

Let
G
be a graph product of countably infinite groups over a strongly reduced finite simple graph
\Gamma
. Let
\mathcal{G}
be a measured groupoid over a standard probability space
X
, equipped with an action-like cocycle
\rho:\mathcal{G}\to G
. Let
\mathcal{Q}\subseteq\mathcal{P}\subseteq\mathcal{G}
be measured subgroupoids, with
(\mathcal{P},\rho)
of product type and nowhere of clique type.

Then
(\mathcal{Q},\rho)
is of clique-inclusive co-factor type within
(\mathcal{P},\rho)
if and only if
(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q})
satisfies Property
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{cofact}})
.

We summarize in
fig.

17
the objects appearing in the proof and the link between them.

Figure 17
:
Objects appearing in Step 1 of the proof of
7.9

Proof.

Step 1
Let us prove that if
(\mathcal{Q},\rho)
is of clique-inclusive co-factor type within
(\mathcal{P},\rho)
, then
(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q})
satisfies
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{cofact}})_{1}
and
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{cofact}})_{2}
.

Notice that if
(\mathcal{Q},\rho)
is of clique-inclusive co-factor type within
(\mathcal{P},\rho)
, then
(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q})
satisfies
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{cofact}})_{1}
, because any co-factor is normal in the ambient product (see Example
4.6
). We now prove that
(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q})
also satisfies Property
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{cofact}})_{2}
.

Up to replacing
X
by a conull Borel subset and partitioning it into countably many Borel subsets, we can (and will) assume that
\mathcal{P}=\rho^{-1}(P)
and
\mathcal{Q}=\rho^{-1}(Q)
, where

—

P=C_{0}\times F_{1}\times\dots\times F_{k}
is a product parabolic subgroup, with (possibly trivial) clique factor
C_{0}
, with
k\geq 1
, and no
F_{i}
decomposes further as a direct product;

—

And
Q=C_{0}\times F_{1}\times\dots\times\hat{F}_{j}\times\dots\times F_{k}
for some
j\in\{1,\dots,k\}
.

We now fix
j\in\{1,\dots,k\}
given by the description of
Q
. Up to a global conjugation, we will also assume that
P=G_{\Lambda}
and
Q=G_{\Upsilon}
for some induced subgraphs
\Upsilon\subseteq\Lambda\subseteq\Gamma
. We let
\Lambda_{j}\subseteq\Lambda
be the subgraph such that
F_{j}=G_{\Lambda_{j}}
– notice that
\Lambda_{j}
is not reduced to a point. We recall from Lemma
4.15
,
4.15
that
(\mathcal{P},\rho)
is tightly
P
-supported.

Since
\Gamma
is strongly reduced, the subgraph
\Lambda_{j}
(which is not reduced to a point) is not collapsible. Therefore, there exists a vertex
v\in V\Lambda_{j}
which is joined to a vertex
w\notin V\Lambda_{j}
, such that
w
is not joined to all vertices of
\Lambda_{j}
. Notice in particular that

G_{w}\subseteq G^{\perp}_{v}\hskip 10.00002pt\text{and}\hskip 10.00002ptG_{w}\nsubseteq F_{j}\times F^{\perp}_{j}\hskip 10.00002pt\text{and}\hskip 10.00002ptG_{w}\nsubseteq P\times P^{\perp}.

(7.1)

Let
\mathcal{B}=\rho^{-1}(G_{v})
. Then
\mathcal{B}
is contained in
\mathcal{P}
, is of infinite type (because
G_{v}
is infinite and
\rho
is action-like) and is normalized by
\mathcal{Q}
(because
G_{v}
is normalized by
Q
, and using Example
4.6
). This proves
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{cofact}})_{2}(b)
.

We now prove
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{cofact}})_{2}(a)
. Let
\mathcal{N}=\rho^{-1}(G_{v}\times G_{v}^{\perp})
, which normalizes
\mathcal{B}
.

To check
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{cofact}})_{2}(a.i)
, let
\mathcal{N}^{\prime}
be a measured subgroupoid of
\mathcal{G}
that stably normalizes
\mathcal{P}
. Without loss of generality, we will assume that
\mathcal{N}^{\prime}
normalizes
\mathcal{P}
. Since
(\mathcal{P},\rho)
is tightly
P
-supported, Lemma
4.17
,
4.17
ensures that
\mathcal{N}^{\prime}\subseteq\rho^{-1}(P\times P^{\perp})
(up to restricting to a conull Borel subset). And
eq.

7.1
implies that
G_{v}\times G_{v}^{\perp}\nsubseteq P\times P^{\perp}
. Lemma
4.14
,
4.14
, thus ensures that for every positive measure Borel subset
U\subseteq X
, one has
\mathcal{N}_{|U}\nsubseteq\mathcal{N}^{\prime}_{|U}
, as desired.

To check
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{cofact}})_{2}(a.ii)
, let
U,\mathcal{S},\mathcal{N}^{\prime},V
be as in the statement. Let
W\subseteq V
be a positive measure Borel subset such that
\mathcal{N}^{\prime}_{|W}
normalizes
\mathcal{S}_{|W}
. Since
(\mathcal{G}_{|W},\mathcal{P}_{|W},\mathcal{S}_{|W})
satisfies Property
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{spec}})
, the second point of Lemma
7.7
ensures that, up to replacing
W
by a positive measure subset,
(\mathcal{S}_{|W},\rho)
is tightly
S
-supported for some special subproduct
S
of
P
. Since by assumption
\mathcal{B}_{|W}=\rho^{-1}(G_{v})_{|W}
is contained in
\mathcal{S}_{|W}
, it follows from Lemma
4.14
,
4.14
that
G_{v}\subseteq S
. So
F_{j}\subseteq S
since
S
is a subproduct. By Lemma
4.17
, up to replacing
W
by a conull Borel subset, we have
\mathcal{N}^{\prime}_{|W}\subseteq\rho^{-1}(S\times S^{\perp})_{|W}
, which in turn is contained in
\rho^{-1}(F_{j}\times F_{j}^{\perp})_{|W}
. The first two inclusions of
eq.

7.1
give that
G_{v}\times G_{v}^{\perp}\nsubseteq F_{j}\times F_{j}^{\perp}
, so Lemma
4.14
implies that
\mathcal{N}_{|W}\nsubseteq\mathcal{N}^{\prime}_{|W}
. And therefore
\mathcal{N}_{|V}\nsubseteq\mathcal{N}^{\prime}_{|V}
, as desired.

Step 2
We now assume that
(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q})
satisfies Properties
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{cofact}})_{1}
and
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{cofact}})_{2}
, and we prove that there exists a measured subgroupoid
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}
of
\mathcal{G}
such that
(\mathcal{Q}^{\prime},\rho)
is of clique-inclusive co-factor type within
(\mathcal{P},\rho)
, and
\mathcal{Q}
is stably contained in
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}
.

Let
\mathcal{B},\mathcal{N}
be as in
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{cofact}})_{2}
. Again, up to passing to a conull Borel subset of
X
and partitioning it into at most countably many Borel subsets, we will assume that
\mathcal{P}=\rho^{-1}(P)
where
P=\mathsf{F}_{1}\times\dots\times\mathsf{F}_{n}
is a product parabolic subgroup of
G
, where the
\mathsf{F}_{i}
are chosen not to split further (in particular the clique subgroup of
P
is decomposed as the product of its vertex groups). We will further assume that
\mathcal{B}
is normalized by
\mathcal{N}
and
\mathcal{Q}
, and that
\mathcal{Q}
is normal in
\mathcal{P}
. Using Lemma
4.16
, we can (and will) also assume that
(\mathcal{B},\rho)
is tightly
B
-supported for some parabolic subgroup
B\subseteq P
, and that
(\mathcal{Q},\rho)
is tightly
Q
-supported for some parabolic subgroup
Q\subseteq P
.

Since
\mathcal{B}
is normalized by
\mathcal{N}
, Lemma
4.17
ensures (up to restricting to a conull Borel subset) that
\mathcal{N}\subseteq\rho^{-1}(B\times B^{\perp})
. For
j\in\{1,\dots,n\}
, let
B_{j}=B\cap\mathsf{F}_{j}
.

We claim that there exists
j\in\{1,\dots,n\}
such that
B_{j}\neq\{1\}
and
B_{j}\neq\mathsf{F}_{j}
. Indeed, otherwise
B
would be a subproduct. But in this case, if
B
was a nonspecial subproduct, then
B\times B^{\perp}\subseteq P\times P^{\perp}
, so
\mathcal{N}\subseteq\rho^{-1}(B\times B^{\perp})\subseteq\rho^{-1}(P\times P^{\perp})
. Since
\mathcal{N}^{\prime}:=\rho^{-1}(P\times P^{\perp})
normalizes
\mathcal{P}
, this gives a contradiction to Property
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{cofact}})_{2}(a.i)
. If
B
was special, then
(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{P},\rho^{-1}(B))
would satisfy Property
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{spec}})
(Lemma
7.7
), and the inclusion
\mathcal{N}\subseteq\rho^{-1}(B\times B^{\perp})
would give a contradiction to Property
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{cofact}})_{2}(a.ii)
, applied this time to
\mathcal{N}^{\prime}:=\rho^{-1}(B\times B^{\perp})
. Hence our claim.

Let
j\in\{1,\dots,n\}
be as above. The above claim shows that
\mathsf{F}_{j}
contains
B_{j}
as a proper parabolic subgroup, so necessarily
\mathsf{F}_{j}
is not conjugate to a vertex group. Since
\mathcal{B}
is normalized by
\mathcal{Q}
, we have
\mathcal{Q}\subseteq\rho^{-1}(B\times B^{\perp})
(Lemma
4.17
), so
Q\subseteq B\times B^{\perp}
. In particular
Q\cap\mathsf{F}_{j}
is contained in
B_{j}\times B_{j}^{\perp}
. So
Q\subseteq\mathsf{F}_{1}\times\dots\times\mathsf{F}_{j-1}\times((B_{j}\times B_{j}^{\perp})\cap\mathsf{F}_{j})\times\mathsf{F}_{j+1}\times\dots\times\mathsf{F}_{n}
. Since
\mathcal{Q}
is normal in
\mathcal{P}=\rho^{-1}(P)
and
(\mathcal{Q},\rho)
is tightly
Q
-supported, we have
P\subseteq Q\times Q^{\perp}
, and we deduce that
Q\subseteq\mathsf{F}_{1}\times\dots\times\hat{\mathsf{F}}_{j}\times\dots\times\mathsf{F}_{n}
. Let
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}=\rho^{-1}(\mathsf{F}_{1}\times\dots\times\hat{\mathsf{F}}_{j}\times\dots\times\mathsf{F}_{n})
. Since
\mathsf{F}_{j}
is not conjugate to a vertex group, the subgroup
\mathsf{F}_{1}\times\dots\times\hat{\mathsf{F}}_{j}\times\dots\times\mathsf{F}_{n}
is a clique-inclusive co-factor, and therefore
(\mathcal{Q}^{\prime},\rho)
is of clique-inclusive co-factor type within
(\mathcal{P},\rho)
. So Step 1 of this proof shows that
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}
satisfies Properties
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{cofact}})_{1}
and
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{cofact}})_{2}
. Moreover
\mathcal{Q}\subseteq\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}
(up to a conull Borel subset). Hence the wanted assertion.

Step 3
We now assume that
(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q})
satisfies Property
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{cofact}})
, and prove that
(\mathcal{Q},\rho)
is of clique-inclusive co-factor type within
(\mathcal{P},\rho)
.

Step 2 gives a measured subgroupoid
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}
of
\mathcal{G}
such that
(\mathcal{Q}^{\prime},\rho)
is of clique-inclusive type within
(\mathcal{P},\rho)
, and
\mathcal{Q}
is stably contained in
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}
. Step 1 then ensures that
(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q}^{\prime})
satisfies
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{cofact}})_{1}
and
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{cofact}})_{2}
. And Assertion
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{cofact}})_{3}
then implies that
\mathcal{Q}
is stably equal to
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}
, showing that
(\mathcal{Q},\rho)
is of clique-inclusive co-factor type within
(\mathcal{P},\rho)
.

Step 4
We are left with showing that if
(\mathcal{Q},\rho)
is of clique-inclusive co-factor type within
(\mathcal{P},\rho)
, then
(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q})
satisfies
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{cofact}})_{3}
.

So assume that
\mathcal{Q}=\rho^{-1}(Q)
for some clique-inclusive co-factor
Q
of
P
, and let
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}
be a measured subgroupoid of
\mathcal{P}
such that
(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q}^{\prime})
satisfies
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{cofact}})_{1}
and
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{cofact}})_{2}
, such that
\mathcal{Q}
is stably contained in
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}
. By Step 2 applied to the triple
(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q}^{\prime})
, up to a conull Borel subset and a countable partition of the base space,
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}
is contained in
\rho^{-1}(Q^{\prime})
for some clique-inclusive co-factor
Q^{\prime}
of
P
. Lemma
4.14
implies that
Q\subseteq Q^{\prime}
, and since
Q
and
Q^{\prime}
are two co-factors of
P
it follows that
Q=Q^{\prime}
, which concludes our proof.
∎

Factors.

Let
\mathcal{G}
be a measured groupoid over a standard probability space
X
, and let
\rho:\mathcal{G}\to G
be an action-like cocycle. Let
\mathcal{P}
be a measured subgroupoid of
\mathcal{G}
such that
(\mathcal{P},\rho)
is of parabolic type. Let
X^{*}=\sqcup_{i\in I}X_{i}
be a partition of a conull Borel subset
X^{*}\subseteq X
into at most countably many Borel subsets, and for every
i\in I
, let
P_{i}
be a parabolic subgroup of
G
, such that
\mathcal{P}_{|X_{i}}=\rho^{-1}(P_{i})_{|X_{i}}
. Let
\mathcal{F}
be a measured subgroupoid of
\mathcal{P}
. We say that
(\mathcal{F},\rho)
is
of factor type within
(\mathcal{P},\rho)
if, up to passing to a further conull Borel subset and refining the above partition, for every
i\in I
, there exists a factor
F_{i}
of
P_{i}
such that
\mathcal{F}_{|X_{i}}=\rho^{-1}(F_{i})_{|X_{i}}
. We say that
(\mathcal{F},\rho)
is
of clique factor type within
(\mathcal{P},\rho)
if additionally, for every
i\in I
such that
X_{i}
has positive measure,
F_{i}
is the clique factor of
P_{i}
.

We now aim to characterize these notions using a purely groupoid-theoretic property, which is as follows.

Definition 7.10

(Property
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{fact}})
)
.

Let
\mathcal{G}
be a measured groupoid over a standard probability space
X
. Let
\mathcal{F}\subseteq\mathcal{P}
be measured subgroupoids of
\mathcal{G}
. We say that the triple
(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{P},\mathcal{F})
satisfies
Property
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{fact}})
if the following three properties hold.

(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{fact}})_{1}

\mathcal{F}
is of infinite type.

(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{fact}})_{2}

There exist
n\geq 1
and measured subgroupoids
\mathcal{Q}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{Q}_{n}
of
\mathcal{P}
such that
(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q}_{j})
satisfies Property
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{cofact}})
for every
j\in\{1,\dots,n\}
, and a countable Borel partition
X^{*}=\sqcup_{i\in I}X_{i}
of a conull Borel subset
X^{*}\subseteq X
such that for every
i\in I
, one has
\mathcal{F}_{|X_{i}}=(\mathcal{Q}_{1}\cap\dots\cap\mathcal{Q}_{n})_{|X_{i}}
.

(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{fact}})_{3}

For every measured subgroupoid
\mathcal{Q}
of
\mathcal{P}
such that
(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q})
satisfies Property
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{cofact}})
, and every positive measure Borel subset
U\subseteq X
, there exists a positive measure Borel subset
V\subseteq U
such that either
\mathcal{F}_{|V}\subseteq\mathcal{Q}_{|V}
or
(\mathcal{F}\cap\mathcal{Q})_{|V}
is trivial.

The idea behind Property
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{fact}})
is the following. If
P=F_{1}\times\dots\times F_{n}
is a product parabolic subgroup with trivial clique factor, then the factors
F_{j}
are characterized as the intersections of co-factors (translated in
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{fact}})_{2}
) which are infinite (as in
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{fact}})_{1}
) and minimal (minimality is the aim of
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{fact}})_{3}
). And if
P
has a non-trivial clique factor, then all clique-inclusive co-factors contain it, and therefore the clique factor is characterized as being the intersection of all clique-inclusive co-factors. The following lemma expresses this idea at the groupoid-theoretic level.

Lemma 7.11
.

Let
G
be a graph product of countably infinite groups over a strongly reduced finite simple graph
\Gamma
. Let
\mathcal{G}
be a measured groupoid over a standard probability space
X
, equipped with an action-like cocycle
\rho:\mathcal{G}\to G
. Let
P\subseteq G
be a product parabolic subgroup with clique factor
C_{0}
, with
P\neq C_{0}
, and let
\mathcal{P}=\rho^{-1}(P)
. Let
\mathcal{F}\subseteq\mathcal{P}
be a measured subgroupoid.

1.

If
C_{0}=\{1\}
, then
(\mathcal{F},\rho)
is of factor type within
(\mathcal{P},\rho)
if and only if
(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{P},\mathcal{F})
satisfies Property
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{fact}})
.

2.

If
C_{0}\neq\{1\}
, then
(\mathcal{F},\rho)
is of clique factor type within
(\mathcal{P},\rho)
if and only if
(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{P},\mathcal{F})
satisfies Property
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{fact}})
.

Proof.

We first assume that
C_{0}=\{1\}
and that
(\mathcal{F},\rho)
is of factor type within
(\mathcal{P},\rho)
. Write
P=F_{1}\times\dots\times F_{n}
, where the factors
F_{j}
do not further decompose as products. Up to a conull Borel subset and a countable partition, and a permutation of the factors
F_{j}
, we will assume that
\mathcal{F}=\rho^{-1}(F_{1})
. In particular
\mathcal{F}
is of infinite type because
F_{1}
is infinite and
\rho
is action-like, and hence
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{fact}})_{1}
is verified. Now, for every
j\in\{2,\dots,k\}
, let
Q_{j}=F_{1}\times\dots\times\hat{F}_{j}\times\dots\times F_{k}
, and let
\mathcal{Q}_{j}=\rho^{-1}(Q_{j})
. Lemma
7.9
,
7.9
ensures that
(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q}_{j})
satisfies Property
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{cofact}})
. In addition
\mathcal{F}=\mathcal{Q}_{2}\cap\dots\cap\mathcal{Q}_{k}
, showing that
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{fact}})_{2}
holds. Finally, let
\mathcal{Q}
be a measured subgroupoid of
\mathcal{P}
such that
(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q})
satisfies
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{cofact}})
. By Lemma
7.9
, there exist a positive measure Borel subset
U\subseteq X
and a clique-inclusive co-factor
Q\subseteq P
such that
\mathcal{Q}_{|U}=\rho^{-1}(Q)_{|U}
. Either
Q=F_{2}\times\dots\times F_{n}
, in which case
(\mathcal{F}\cap\mathcal{Q})_{|U}
is trivial, or else
F_{1}\subseteq Q
, in which case
\mathcal{F}_{|U}\subseteq\mathcal{Q}_{|U}
. This shows that
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{fact}})_{3}
holds.

We now assume that
C_{0}\neq\{1\}
and that
(\mathcal{F},\rho)
is of clique factor type within
(\mathcal{P},\rho)
. Write
P=C_{0}\times F_{1}\times\dots\times F_{k}
, where the
F_{j}
do not further decompose as direct products. Up to a conull Borel subset and a countable partition, we will assume that
\mathcal{F}=\rho^{-1}(C_{0})
. In particular
\mathcal{F}
is of infinite type because
C_{0}
is infinite and
\rho
is action-like, showing that
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{fact}})_{1}
holds. For every
j\in\{1,\dots,k\}
, let
Q_{j}=C_{0}\times F_{1}\times\dots\times\hat{F}_{j}\times\dots\times F_{k}
, and let
\mathcal{Q}_{j}=\rho^{-1}(Q_{j})
. Lemma
7.9
ensures that
(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q}_{j})
satisfies Property
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{cofact}})
. In addition
\mathcal{F}=\mathcal{Q}_{1}\cap\dots\cap\mathcal{Q}_{k}
, showing that
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{fact}})_{2}
holds. Finally, let
\mathcal{Q}
be a measured subgroupoid of
\mathcal{P}
such that
(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q})
satisfies
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{cofact}})
. By Lemma
7.9
, for every positive measure Borel subset
U\subseteq X
there exist a positive measure Borel subset
V\subseteq U
and a clique-inclusive co-factor
Q\subseteq P
such that
\mathcal{Q}_{|V}=\rho^{-1}(Q)_{|V}
. Then
C_{0}\subseteq Q
, so
\mathcal{F}_{|V}\subseteq\mathcal{Q}_{|V}
, showing that
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{fact}})_{3}
holds.

Conversely, let us now assume that
(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{P},\mathcal{F})
satisfies Property
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{fact}})
. Let
\mathcal{Q}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{Q}_{n}
be measured subgroupoids of
\mathcal{P}
as in
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{fact}})_{2}
. Up to a conull Borel subset and a countable partition, we will assume that
\mathcal{F}=\mathcal{Q}_{1}\cap\dots\cap\mathcal{Q}_{n}
, and, using Lemma
7.9
, that
\mathcal{Q}_{j}=\rho^{-1}(Q_{j})
for some clique-inclusive co-factor
Q_{j}
. Thus
\mathcal{F}=\rho^{-1}(F)
, where
F=Q_{1}\cap\dots\cap Q_{n}
is a subproduct of
P
. Then
F
is non-trivial as
\mathcal{F}
is of infinite type by
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{fact}})_{1}
while
\rho
has trivial kernel, and
F
contains
C_{0}
. To conclude the proof, there only remains to show that

—

if
C_{0}=\{1\}
, then
F
does not split as a product of at least two factors;

—

if
C_{0}\neq\{1\}
, then
F
does not split as a product of
C_{0}
and at least another factor.

But in both cases, if it did, then we could find a clique-inclusive co-factor
Q
such that
F\cap Q
is an infinite proper parabolic subgroup of
F
(in particular
F\nsubseteq Q
). We now reach a contradiction to
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{fact}})_{3}
by letting
\mathcal{Q}=\rho^{-1}(Q)
: indeed for every positive measure Borel subset
U\subseteq X
, Lemma
4.14
shows that
\mathcal{F}_{|U}\nsubseteq\mathcal{Q}_{|U}
, while the fact that
F\cap Q
is infinite and
\rho
is action-like ensures that
(\mathcal{F}\cap\mathcal{Q})_{|U}
is non-trivial. This completes our proof.
∎

7.4
Proof of the Vertex Recognition Property

We finally introduce the property that will enable us to characterize subgroupoids of vertex type
4
4

4

Recall that groupoids of vertex type are defined in
section

5.1
,
section

5.1
with no reference to an action-like cocycle, under the condition that the defining graph of
G
is transvection-free and strongly reduced. We first state the property, and then comment it below – it is inspired from Lemma
6.6
.

Definition 7.12

(Property
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{vert}})
)
.

Let
\mathcal{G}
be a measured groupoid over a standard probability space
X
, and let
\mathcal{V}
be a measured subgroupoid of
\mathcal{G}
. We say that the pair
(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{V})
satisfies
Property
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{vert}})
if
\mathcal{V}
is of infinite type and the following four properties hold.

(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{vert}})_{1}

There exists a countable Borel partition
X^{*}=\sqcup_{i\in I}X_{i}
of a conull Borel subset
X^{*}\subseteq X
such that for every
i\in I
, there exist measured subgroupoids

\mathcal{G}_{|X_{i}}=\mathcal{F}_{0}\supseteq\mathcal{P}_{1}\supseteq\mathcal{F}_{1}\supseteq\mathcal{P}_{2}\supseteq\mathcal{F}_{2}\supseteq\dots\supseteq\mathcal{P}_{n}\supseteq\mathcal{F}_{n}=\mathcal{V}_{|X_{i}}

of
\mathcal{G}_{|X_{i}}
(with
n\geq 1
) such that

(a)

for every
j\in\{1,\dots,n\}
, the pair
(\mathcal{F}_{j-1},\mathcal{P}_{j})
satisfies Property
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{prod}})
;

(b)

for every
j\in\{1,\dots,n\}
, the triple
(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{P}_{j},\mathcal{F}_{j})
satisfies Property
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{fact}})
.

(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{vert}})_{2}

For every measured subgroupoid
\mathcal{W}
of
\mathcal{G}
of infinite type which is stably contained in
\mathcal{V}
, and every measured subgroupoid
\mathcal{H}\subseteq\mathcal{G}
, if
\mathcal{W}
is stably normalized by
\mathcal{H}
, then
\mathcal{V}
is stably normalized by
\mathcal{H}
.

(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{vert}})_{3}

There exists a measured subgroupoid
\mathcal{N}
of
\mathcal{G}
that stably normalizes
\mathcal{V}
, such that for every measured subgroupoid
\mathcal{V}^{\prime}
of
\mathcal{G}
such that
(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{V}^{\prime})
satisfies Properties
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{vert}})_{1}
and
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{vert}})_{2}
, every measured subgroupoid
\mathcal{N}^{\prime}
of
\mathcal{G}
that stably normalizes
\mathcal{V}^{\prime}
, and every positive measure Borel subset
U\subseteq X
, there exists a positive measure Borel subset
V\subseteq U
such that either
\mathcal{N}_{|V}=\mathcal{N}^{\prime}_{|V}
or
\mathcal{N}_{|V}\nsubseteq\mathcal{N}^{\prime}_{|V}
.

(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{vert}})_{4}

Whenever
\mathcal{V}^{\prime}
is a measured subgroupoid of
\mathcal{G}
such that
(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{V}^{\prime})
satisfies Properties
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{vert}})_{1},(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{vert}})_{2}
and
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{vert}})_{3}
, if
\mathcal{V}
is stably contained in
\mathcal{V}^{\prime}
, then they are stably equal.

A few comments regarding the meaning of this property are in order. Property
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{vert}})_{1}
is a groupoid translation of the contents of Lemma
6.6
,
lemma

6.6
, saying informally that vertex groups can be obtained by successively passing to maximal product parabolic subgroups and their factors. However, if we only considered Property
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{vert}})_{1}
and end this process too early, we could end up with a subgroupoid “of parabolic type”, i.e. of the form
\rho^{-1}(B)
for some parabolic subgroup
B\subseteq G
(possibly after taking a conull subset and a countable Borel partition), where
B
could be larger than a vertex group. Also, because of the slight asymmetry in our lemma that deals with subgroupoids of maximal product type (Lemma
7.4
,
7.4
), we could possibly end up with a subgroupoid contained in one “of clique type” (possibly, a subgroupoid strictly contained in one of vertex type), with no precise control. The role of Properties
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{vert}})_{2}
,
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{vert}})_{3}
and
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{vert}})_{4}
is to exclude these extra possibilities, as we now explain.

Under our assumption that the defining graph
\Gamma
is strongly reduced, a parabolic subgroup
B
that is not conjugate to a vertex group, always contains a proper (parabolic) subgroup
A\subseteq B
with
N_{G}(A)\nsubseteq N_{G}(B)
. On the other hand a vertex group does not. Property
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{vert}})_{2}
is the groupoid translation of this fact, which will allow us to exclude subgroupoids of parabolic type but not of vertex type.

Assuming that
\Gamma
is transvection-free, if
B
is a proper subgroup of a clique subgroup
C
, then the normalizer of
B
is strictly contained in the normalizer of any vertex subgroup of
C
. On the other hand, the normalizer of a vertex group is not strictly contained in the normalizer of any other vertex group. A groupoid version of this distinction is exploited in Property
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{vert}})_{3}
, to exclude subgroupoids contained in one “of clique type” but not contained in one of vertex type.

Finally, a maximality assumption is exploited in Property
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{vert}})_{4}
to exclude proper subgroupoids of subgroupoids of vertex type.

Having explained the general idea behind it, we can now turn to the proof of our main lemma, from which the Vertex Recognition Property (see
5.1
) follows.

Lemma 7.13
.

Let
G
be a graph product of countably infinite groups over a strongly reduced transvection-free finite simple graph
\Gamma
not reduced to a vertex.
Let
\mathcal{G}
be a measured groupoid over a standard probability space
X
, equipped with an action-like cocycle
\rho:\mathcal{G}\to G
. Let
\mathcal{V}\subseteq\mathcal{G}
be a measured subgroupoid.

Then
(\mathcal{V},\rho)
is of vertex type if and only if
(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{V})
satisfies Property
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{vert}})
.

Proof.

The proof has four steps.

Step 1.
Let us show that if
(\mathcal{V},\rho)
is of vertex type, then
\mathcal{V}
is of infinite type and
(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{V})
satisfies
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{vert}})_{1}
and
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{vert}})_{2}
.

Since
\rho
is action-like and
(\mathcal{V},\rho)
is of vertex type, and since vertex groups are infinite,
\mathcal{V}
is of infinite type. Up to passing to a conull Borel subset, taking a countable partition, and conjugating the cocycle, we can assume that
\mathcal{V}=\rho^{-1}(G_{v})
for some vertex
v\in V\Gamma
. The vertex
v
is untransvectable because
\Gamma
is transvection-free.

For
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{vert}})_{1}
, by Lemma
6.6
,
lemma

6.6
, there exists a chain of parabolic subgroups

G=F_{0}\supseteq P_{1}\supseteq F_{1}\supseteq P_{2}\supseteq F_{2}\supseteq\dots\supseteq P_{n}\supseteq F_{n}=G_{v}

such that

1.

for every
j\in\{1,\dots,n\}
,
P_{j}
is a maximal product parabolic subgroup of
F_{j-1}
which is not of isolated clique type, and

2.

for every
j\in\{1,\dots,n-1\}
, the clique factor of
P_{j}
is trivial, and
F_{j}
is a factor of
P_{j}
, and

3.

F_{n}
is the clique factor of
P_{n}
.

For every
j\in\{1,\dots,n\}
, let
\mathcal{P}_{j}=\rho^{-1}(P_{j})
, and let
\mathcal{F}_{j}=\rho^{-1}(F_{j})
. These come equipped with action-like cocycles to
P_{j},F_{j}
, respectively. Notice that for every
j\in\{1,\dots,n\}
, the pair
(\mathcal{P}_{j},\rho)
is nowhere of isolated clique type. By the first assertion of Lemma
7.4
,
7.4
, for every
j\in\{1,\dots,n\}
, the pair
(\mathcal{F}_{j-1},\mathcal{P}_{j})
satisfies Property
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{prod}})
. By Lemma
7.11
, using that
\Gamma
is strongly reduced, for every
j\in\{1,\dots,n\}
, the triple
(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{P}_{j},\mathcal{F}_{j})
satisfies Property
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{fact}})
.

For
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{vert}})_{2}
, let
\mathcal{W}
be a measured subgroupoid of infinite type which is stably contained in
\mathcal{V}
, and let
\mathcal{H}\subseteq\mathcal{G}
be a measured subgroupoid that stably normalizes
\mathcal{W}
. Up to a countable partition of the base space, we will assume that
\mathcal{W}\subseteq\mathcal{V}
and that
\mathcal{H}
normalizes
\mathcal{W}
. Then
(\mathcal{W},\rho)
is tightly
G_{v}
-supported, so Lemma
4.17
,
4.17
, ensures (up to restricting to a conull Borel subset) that
\mathcal{H}\subseteq\rho^{-1}(G_{v}\times G_{v}^{\perp})
. Therefore
\mathcal{H}
normalizes
\mathcal{V}=\rho^{-1}(G_{v})
, as desired.

Step 2.
Let us show that if
(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{V})
satisfies Properties
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{vert}})_{1}
and
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{vert}})_{2}
, then there exist a countable Borel partition
X^{*}=\sqcup_{i\in I}X_{i}
of a conull Borel subset
X^{*}\subseteq X
, and for every
i\in I
, a parabolic subgroup
C_{i}
that is conjugate to
G_{\Upsilon_{i}}
, for some non-empty complete subgraph
\Upsilon_{i}\subseteq\Gamma
(possibly reduced to one vertex), such that
\mathcal{V}_{|X_{i}}\subseteq\rho^{-1}(C_{i})_{|X_{i}}
.

Up to a countable partition, we can assume that there exist subgroupoids

\mathcal{G}=\mathcal{F}_{0}\supseteq\mathcal{P}_{1}\supseteq\mathcal{F}_{1}\supseteq\mathcal{P}_{2}\supseteq\mathcal{F}_{2}\supseteq\dots\supseteq\mathcal{P}_{n}\supseteq\mathcal{F}_{n}=\mathcal{V}

as in
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{vert}})_{1}
. By inductively applying Lemmas
7.4
and
7.11
, we show that for every
i\in\{1,\dots,n\}
, up to passing to a conull Borel subset and taking a countable partition,

—

Either
\mathcal{P}_{i}=\rho^{-1}(P_{i})
for some maximal product parabolic subgroup
P_{i}
whose type is not a clique, and
\mathcal{F}_{i}=\rho^{-1}(F_{i})
for some factor
F_{i}
of
P_{i}
;

—

Or
\mathcal{P}_{i}\subseteq\rho^{-1}(C)
for some parabolic subgroup
C
that is conjugate to
G_{\Upsilon}
for some non-empty complete subgraph
\Upsilon\subseteq\Gamma
.

In particular, either
\mathcal{V}=\rho^{-1}(B)
for some proper parabolic subgroup
B
, or else
\mathcal{V}\subseteq\rho^{-1}(C)
for some parabolic group
C
that is conjugate to
G_{\Upsilon}
for some non-empty complete subgraph
\Upsilon\subseteq\Gamma
.
To show the wanted assertion, it suffices to exclude the case where
\mathcal{V}=\rho^{-1}(B)
for a parabolic subgroup
B
whose type is an induced subgraph
\Lambda\subseteq\Gamma
that contains at least
2
vertices. Up to conjugating the cocycle we will assume that
\mathcal{V}=\rho^{-1}(G_{\Lambda})
and aim for a contradiction. Since
\Gamma
is strongly reduced, there exists a vertex
w\in V\Lambda
such that
G_{w}\times G_{w}^{\perp}\nsubseteq G_{\Lambda}\times G_{\Lambda}^{\perp}
, as otherwise the subgraph
\Lambda
would be collapsible. We will now use Property
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{vert}})_{2}
to reach a contradiction. For this, let
\mathcal{W}=\rho^{-1}(G_{w})
. Then
\mathcal{W}
is contained in
\mathcal{V}
, and
\mathcal{W}
is of infinite type because
G_{w}
is infinite and
\rho
is action-like. Let
\mathcal{H}=\rho^{-1}(G_{w}\times G_{w}^{\perp})
, which normalizes
\mathcal{W}
(Example
4.6
,
4.6
). So Property
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{vert}})_{2}
implies that
\mathcal{V}
is stably normalized by
\mathcal{H}
. As
(\mathcal{V},\rho)
is tightly
G_{\Lambda}
-supported (Lemma
4.15
,
4.15
), it follows from Lemma
4.17
,
4.17
that
\mathcal{H}\subseteq\rho^{-1}(G_{\Lambda}\times G_{\Lambda}^{\perp})
. But since
G_{w}\times G_{w}^{\perp}\nsubseteq G_{\Lambda}\times G_{\Lambda}^{\perp}
, this is a contradiction to Lemma
4.14
,
4.14
. This contradiction completes our proof of Step 2.

Step 3.
Let us show that if
(\mathcal{V},\rho)
is of vertex type, then
(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{V})
satisfies Property
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{vert}})_{3}
.

Again we assume that
\mathcal{V}=\rho^{-1}(G_{v})
for some
v\in V\Gamma
. Let
\mathcal{N}=\rho^{-1}(G_{v}\times G_{v}^{\perp})
, which normalizes
\mathcal{V}
(Example
4.6
,
4.6
). Let
\mathcal{V}^{\prime}
be a measured subgroupoid of
\mathcal{G}
that satisfies
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{vert}})_{1}
and
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{vert}})_{2}
, let
\mathcal{N}^{\prime}
be a measured subgroupoid of
\mathcal{G}
that stably normalizes
\mathcal{V}^{\prime}
and let
U\subseteq X
be a positive measure Borel subset. By Lemma
4.16
,
4.16
, we can find a positive measure Borel subset
V\subseteq U
such that
(\mathcal{V}^{\prime}_{|V},\rho)
is tightly
C
-supported for some parabolic subgroup
C\subseteq G
. Up to partitioning, we can (and will) also assume that
\mathcal{N}^{\prime}_{|V}
normalizes
\mathcal{V}^{\prime}_{|V}
. By the previous step,
C
is conjugate to
G_{\Upsilon}
for some non-empty complete subgraph
\Upsilon\subseteq\Gamma
. Since
v
is untransvectable, it follows from Lemma
2.9
,
2.9
, that either
C=G_{v}
, or else
G_{v}\times G_{v}^{\perp}\nsubseteq C\times C^{\perp}
. If
C=G_{v}
, then Lemma
4.17
,
4.17
ensures that
\mathcal{N}^{\prime}_{|V}\subseteq\mathcal{N}_{|V}
(up to a null set), and we are done. If
G_{v}\times G_{v}^{\perp}\nsubseteq C\times C^{\perp}
, then Lemma
4.14
,
4.14
ensures that
\mathcal{N}_{|V}\nsubseteq\rho^{-1}(C\times C^{\perp})_{|V}
. As
\mathcal{N}^{\prime}_{|V}
normalizes
\mathcal{V}^{\prime}_{|V}
, we also have
\mathcal{N}^{\prime}_{|V}\subseteq\rho^{-1}(C\times C^{\perp})_{|V}
(Lemma
4.17
,
4.17
). So we deduce that
\mathcal{N}_{|V}\nsubseteq\mathcal{N}^{\prime}_{|V}
, and again Property
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{vert}})_{3}
holds.

Step 4.
Let us finally show that if
(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{V})
satisfies Properties
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{vert}})_{1}
,
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{vert}})_{2}
and
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{vert}})_{3}
, then there exist a countable Borel partition
X^{*}=\sqcup_{i\in I}X_{i}
of a conull Borel subset
X^{*}\subseteq X
, and for every
i\in I
, a parabolic subgroup
A_{i}
that is conjugate to a vertex subgroup, such that
\mathcal{V}_{|X_{i}}\subseteq\rho^{-1}(A_{i})_{|X_{i}}
.

Otherwise, in view of Step 2 (and Lemma
4.16
,
4.16
), there would exist a positive measure Borel subset
U\subseteq X
such that
(\mathcal{V}_{|U},\rho)
is tightly
C
-supported for some parabolic subgroup
C
conjugate to
G_{\Upsilon}
, where
\Upsilon\subseteq\Gamma
is a complete subgraph of size at least
2
. Let
\mathcal{N}
be a measured subgroupoid given by
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{vert}})_{3}
. Since
\mathcal{N}
stably normalizes
\mathcal{V}
, Lemma
4.17
,
4.17
, ensures that, up to replacing
U
by a positive measure subset, we have
\mathcal{N}_{|U}\subseteq\rho^{-1}(C\times C^{\perp})_{|U}
. Let
A\subseteq C
be a parabolic subgroup that is equal to a conjugate of a vertex group. Let
\mathcal{V}^{\prime}
be a measured subgroupoid of
\mathcal{G}
such that
\mathcal{V}^{\prime}_{|U}=\rho^{-1}(A)_{|U}
, and
\mathcal{V}^{\prime}_{|X\setminus U}=\mathcal{V}_{|X\setminus U}
. Using Step 1, we see that
(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{V}^{\prime})
satisfies Properties
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{vert}})_{1}
and
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{vert}})_{2}
. Let
\mathcal{N}^{\prime}
be such that
\mathcal{N}^{\prime}_{|U}=\rho^{-1}(A\times A^{\perp})_{|U}
, and
\mathcal{N}^{\prime}_{|X\setminus U}=\mathcal{N}_{|X\setminus U}
. Then
\mathcal{N}^{\prime}
stably normalizes
\mathcal{V}^{\prime}
. As every vertex of
\Gamma
is untransvectable, the inclusion
C\times C^{\perp}\subseteq A\times A^{\perp}
is strict. Lemma
4.14
,
4.14
thus implies that
\mathcal{N}_{|V}\subsetneq\mathcal{N}^{\prime}_{|V}
for every positive measure Borel subset
V\subseteq U
, contradicting
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{vert}})_{3}
.

Conclusion.
If
(\mathcal{V},\rho)
is of vertex type, then
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{vert}})_{1}
,
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{vert}})_{2}
and
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{vert}})_{3}
are verified by Steps 1 and 3. Finally, take
\mathcal{V}^{\prime}
verifying the hypothesis of
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{vert}})_{4}
. By Step 4, up to taking a conull Borel subset and a countable partition, there exists a subgroup
A\subseteq G
that is conjugate to a vertex group, such that
\mathcal{V}^{\prime}\subseteq\rho^{-1}(A)
. Up to refining the partition, we also assume that
\mathcal{V}=\rho^{-1}(B)
for some subgroup
B\subseteq G
that is conjugate to a vertex group. Since
\mathcal{V}
is stably contained in
\mathcal{V}^{\prime}
, we deduce that
\rho^{-1}(B)
is stably contained in
\rho^{-1}(A)
. By Lemma
4.14
,
4.14
, this implies that
B\subseteq A
, and in fact
A=B
since they are both conjugate to vertex groups. Hence
(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{V})
satisfies
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{vert}})
.

Conversely, if
(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{V})
verifies
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{vert}})
then using the maximality assumption provided by Property
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{vert}})_{4}
and the above Step 4, we get that
(\mathcal{V},\rho)
is of vertex type.
∎

We are now in position to complete our proof of Proposition
7.1
.

Proof.

Let
G,H
be two graph products of countably infinite groups over transvection-free strongly reduced finite simple graphs. Let
\mathcal{G}
be a measured groupoid over a standard probability space, equipped with action-like cocycles
\rho_{G}:\mathcal{G}\to G
and
\rho_{H}:\mathcal{G}\to H
. Let
\mathcal{V}\subseteq\mathcal{G}
be a measured subgroupoid, and assume that the pair
(\mathcal{V},\rho_{G})
is of vertex type; we aim to prove that
(\mathcal{V},\rho_{H})
is of vertex type.

By Lemma
7.13
, applied to the cocycle
\rho_{G}
, the pair
(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{V})
satisfies Property
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{vert}})
. And by Lemma
7.13
, applied to the cocycle
\rho_{H}
, the pair
(\mathcal{V},\rho_{H})
is of vertex type, as desired.
∎

8
The reducible case and proof of Proposition
5.3

In this section, we complete our proof of Proposition
5.3
, by treating the case of reducible graph products.

8.1
Goal of the section

The main result of this section is the following proposition, which extends Proposition
7.1
to the case of direct products.

Proposition 8.1
.

The class of graph products of countably infinite groups over finite simple graphs
\Gamma
that splits as a join
\Gamma=\Gamma_{1}\circ\dots\circ\Gamma_{k}
(with
k\geq 1
) of transvection-free strongly reduced graphs
\Gamma_{j}
on at least
2
vertices, satisfies the Vertex Recognition Property.

In view of Corollary
6.4
, this covers all graph products over transvection-free graphs with no partial conjugation, and therefore Proposition
5.3
follows from Proposition
8.1
. In the previous section, we have treated the case where
k=1
, we now need to tackle the reducible case.

8.2
Proof of the Vertex Recognition Property

Given a graph product
G
that splits as a direct product with trivial clique factor, the following property will enable us to recognize the co-factors of the product, at a groupoid-theoretic level.

Definition 8.2

(Property
(\mathrm{P}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{cofact}})
)
.

Let
\mathcal{G}
be a measured groupoid over a standard probability space, and let
\mathcal{Q}
be a measured subgroupoid of
\mathcal{G}
. We say that
(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{Q})
satisfies
Property
(\mathrm{P}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{cofact}})
if the following two properties hold.

(\mathrm{P}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{cofact}})_{1}

The groupoid
\mathcal{Q}
is of infinite type and stably normal in
\mathcal{G}
, and it stably normalizes an amenable measured subgroupoid
\mathcal{A}
of
\mathcal{G}
of infinite type.

(\mathrm{P}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{cofact}})_{2}

Whenever
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}
is a measured subgroupoid of
\mathcal{G}
such that
(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{Q}^{\prime})
satisfies Property
(\mathrm{P}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{cofact}})_{1}
, and such that
\mathcal{Q}
is stably contained in
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}
, then
\mathcal{Q}
and
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}
are stably equal.

The idea behind Property
(\mathrm{P}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{cofact}})
is the following. Assume that a graph product
G
splits non-trivially as a product
G=F_{1}\times\dots\times F_{k}
, with trivial clique factor. Then each
Q_{j}=F_{1}\times\dots\times\hat{F}_{j}\times\dots\times F_{k}
is normal in
G
and normalizes an infinite amenable subgroup
A\subseteq F_{j}
. Conversely, any maximal subgroup of
G
with these properties is a co-factor. The following lemma is the groupoid-theoretic version of this fact (since all clique factors are trivial in this section, for simplicity, we will talk about “subgroupoids of co-factor type” as a shortcut for “subgroupoids of clique-inclusive co-factor type”).

Lemma 8.3
.

Let
G
be a graph product over a finite simple graph
\Gamma
with countably infinite vertex groups and trivial clique factor. Let
\mathcal{G}
be a measured groupoid over a standard probability space
X
, equipped with an action-like cocycle
\rho:\mathcal{G}\to G
. Let
\mathcal{Q}\subseteq\mathcal{G}
be a measured subgroupoid.

Then
(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{Q})
satisfies Property
(\mathrm{P}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{cofact}})
if and only if
G
is reducible and
(\mathcal{Q},\rho)
is of co-factor type within
(\mathcal{G},\rho)
.

Proof.

Let
G=F_{1}\times\dots\times F_{k}
be the decomposition of
G
into its irreducible components (possibly with
k=1
).

Step 1
We first assume that
G
is reducible (i.e.
k\geq 2
) and that
(\mathcal{Q},\rho)
is of co-factor type within
(\mathcal{G},\rho)
, and prove that
(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{Q})
satisfies
(\mathrm{P}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{cofact}})_{1}
.

Up to a conull Borel subset and a countable partition, we will assume that
\mathcal{Q}=\rho^{-1}(Q)
, where
Q=F_{1}\times\dots\times\hat{F}_{j}\times\dots\times F_{k}
for some
j\in\{1,\dots,k\}
. Then
\mathcal{Q}
is of infinite type (because
Q
is infinite and
\rho
is action-like) and normalized by
\mathcal{G}
(because
Q
is normal in
G
). Let
A\subseteq F_{j}
be an infinite amenable subgroup: this always exists, because the type of
F_{j}
(i.e. the underlying subgraph of
\Gamma
) contains at least
2
vertices. The conclusion follows by letting
\mathcal{A}=\rho^{-1}(A)
.

Step 2
Let us prove that if
(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{Q})
satisfies

then

) and

of

is of co-factor type within
(\mathcal{G},\rho)
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be given by

. Without loss of generality, we will assume that
\mathcal{A}
is normalized by
\mathcal{Q}
. For every

, let

be the cocycle obtained by post-composing
\rho
by the projection

. Up to a countable Borel partition of

, we will assume that
(\mathcal{A},\rho)
is tightly
A
-supported for some parabolic subgroup

(and

because
\mathcal{A}
is of infinite type).

\bullet
Let

be such that

(such a

,
2.7
). In particular

is nowhere trivial (by the second point of the definition of tight support,

).

\bullet
Let us prove that
(\rho_{j})_{|\mathcal{Q}}
is stably trivial.

So assume towards a contradiction that
(\rho_{j})_{|\mathcal{Q}}
is not stably trivial.

—

We first show that there exists a Borel subset
V
of positive measure such that
\rho_{j}
is nowhere trivial on
\mathcal{Q}_{|V}
.

Let
U\subseteq X
be a Borel subset of maximal measure such that
\rho_{j}
is stably trivial on

. Our assumption ensures that
V=X\setminus U
has positive measure. And
\rho_{j}
is nowhere trivial on
\mathcal{Q}_{|V}
.

—

We now show that
(\rho^{-1}(F_{j})_{|V},\rho_{j})
is

-elementary (in the sense of

).

Lemma

,

are

is

-elementary.

—

Since

,

.

This contradiction shows that

is stably trivial.

\bullet
Let us show that

is reducible.

Assume that

is irreducible, namely that

. Then
\rho_{1}=\rho
and since

).

\bullet
Taking

and

gives the wanted conclusion for Step 2.

Conclusion
If

is reducible and
(\mathcal{Q},\rho)
is of co-factor type within
(\mathcal{G},\rho)
, then it verifies
(\mathrm{P}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{cofact}})_{1}
by Step 1. To check

take
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}
such that

with

.
By Step

, there exists

such that

. Since an inclusion of co-factors is always an equality, we deduce that

are stably equal.

Now assume that

. By Step 2,

for

is of co-factor type within

.
∎

We now consider the following property which is a very slight variation over Property
(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{fact}})
from the previous section (Definition
